ParlaMint-AT_2005-07-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00116_d7e6052	In the presidential conference, consensus was reached on the design and duration of the debates. Accordingly, a day block time of 9 ‘Vienna hours’ has been agreed, so that the following speaking times arise: ÖVP and SPÖ each 158 minutes, Free 108 minutes and Green 117 minutes. The following Rules of Procedure were also adopted for the debate between 10.10 and 1 p.m. and 1 p.m., which was transmitted by the ORF: first, a statement by a political group of 15 minutes, then a statement by a member of the government with 15 minutes, then a statement by a group of 10 minutes, then a statement by a member of the government by 10 minutes, then a statement by a group of 5 minutes, and finally one word per group of 5 minutes. Before the end of the final round, the remaining speaking time will be equally distributed between the President-in-Office of the Council and the political groups in such a way that all political groups can still speak. There is also agreement that actual corrections will only be called after the television broadcast. The House shall decide on this Rules of Procedure and Agreement. ParlaMint-AT_2005-07-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00116_d7e7240	Madam President. Federal Chancellor! Mrs Governmentary colleague! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. High house! At my entry speech on 22. December last year I defined two major priorities for the security of our country. What should and must happen in this legislative period was, on the one hand, the creation of a modern, uniform police force and, on the other hand, the creation of an effective and at the same time human asylum system, embedded in a corresponding foreign police framework. We have tried very hard to discuss these issues of security on the broadest possible basis, and it will now be up to us to implement them as widely as possible. A week ago, the new police became reality. It is the largest modernisation in the history of executives, and today a new asylum law, a new foreign police law, a new law of establishment and residence is to be adopted by the National Council. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] This law has not, as we have heard in the media in recent days, weeks and months, been created in the “quiet chamber” but has been discussed on a broad basis, and it is not a theoretical law and concept either. We have deliberately involved very wide in all the processes of all those areas that are entrusted with the execution, which have known and analyzed the execution precisely. We have involved a number of practitioners, including NGOs, in this discussion and have received proposals for improvement. We have constantly contacted excellent legal experts, and finally, in a wide, also very open discussion with the representatives of all parties, we have tried to work out this issue and make it a law that also finds broad consensus. I'm pleased about this broad consensus. I think this is a necessary measure. Above all, we have clearly seen in the necessary objective in the area of asylum and migration that there is consensus across the parties and organisations, across the expert series. This consensus is simple: Help on the issue of aid, stop where it is about abuse – and there is abuse, Mr Van der Bellen; we do not generalize it, but there is abuse – and punishment about crime. And on this consensus the law builds. Firstly, we want to create as human framework as possible, and there are still many measures needed. Above all, I would like to stress: A push not “hafen”, but we will try to install feedback centres in such a way that they are human. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] It is also a matter of giving rapid help. The second point, which is very important, is that we can offer our citizens as much security as possible, where it is about abuse, where it is also about criminal acts by means of tow or other criminals. But we want and we also need to speed up those abuses by people who misuse asylum as so-called economic refugees. It is very important that we develop effective measures and procedures for those asylum seekers who become criminal. Last year alone, we had 12,000 suspects who also sought asylum. I would like to thank all those who have contributed to this consensus: for the need for security policy, but also for serving humanity. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] I know it's not easy for a big opposition party to go along with this. But I say thank you for this really important discussion. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Ladies and gentlemen! As long as there are lawyers, there will also be different legal opinions in sensitive areas. And there is a need for political responsibility to make decisions with particular care and consideration of all these opinions. That's our job. And we will now, in practice with a lot of sensitivity, but also transparency, ensure that this is implemented accordingly. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] We had this task in this package of asylum law, foreign police law, establishment and residence law. In my opinion, this is the most comprehensive act that ever existed on this matter. That is why I also believe, Mrs Stoisits, that it is very, very important that we combine all these issues, because in the discussion with the parties, with NGOs, churches and stakeholders, we have seen that it flows together, that we are right and that these areas are intertwined like gears. They can therefore only be dealt with together in a satisfactory manner. We need clear provisions on the condition that foreigners are granted residence and establishment in Austria and that they are entitled to do so. However, we also need – and this is very important – improved integration, especially in the language, and this will be included in the integration agreement. We need the best possible and rapid integration of those immigrants who are legal and permanent in Austria. As these materials are so closely linked, it is all the more important to make a distinction between asylum and migration. Those who mix these two areas in terms of content are harming those who really need help for persecution, and are also harming the legitimate need for security for our citizens. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] We have tried to pay close attention to this separation between asylum, protection for persecuted people, migration and global migration for the various reasons of non-protection people. In the beginning, we will try to achieve two goals: firstly, the really great tradition of helpfulness in our country – we have repeatedly demonstrated it, and it will continue to be as possible – and secondly, the fulfilment of the legitimate security interests of our citizens. Austria is already doing violent things today; they have already been called numbers. At present, there are about 28 000 people each year who are being cared for with NGOs in Austria together with the countries. UNHCR statistics also show that Austria with three asylum seekers per 1 000 inhabitants worldwide is absolutely in the forefront. It is, therefore, really my heart's desire today to thank the Austrians for their helpfulness, but also for their willingness to welcome strangers. Finally, I would like to thank all those who have accompanied this path of humanity and security: the high-level legal experts involved, the practitioners, the NGOs practitioners, the administrative staff – there have been many ministries working together. Last but not least, I would like to thank the politicians of the various parties in this House very much. It was a constructive, sometimes a very Hard but very intense discussion, which we have held over long distances; but I thought it was very important. I now hope that, in implementing this law, we can continue this broad path of consensus in the future.

More than 8.5 percent of asylum applications in the EU were submitted in Austria – with only 2% of the population. Also in the first quarter of this year, we have made applications according to the UN refugee organisation UNHCR 4 222. With the 2005 Foreign Rights Pact, we have achieved an important step towards fair treatment in the particularly sensitive area of asylum and xenophobia. As a human rights spokesman, I am particularly concerned that the new law is in line with the Geneva Convention on Refugees and the European Convention on Human Rights and, of course, is also constitutional. The premise of the new asylum law is that there is a clear distinction between those who need help and those who misuse help. [[Demonstrativ accompaniment of the Abg. Prince.]] For the latter, effective legal provisions and instruments must be available in order to be able to object to abuse through incorrect information of identity, nationality or by presenting false documents and the like. By the way, this is also the attitude of our people; and I thank the people of Austria very much for this attitude. In order to further facilitate the work of the asylum authorities, a country documentation will be established to which the Austrian authorities and courts can access to work with the same data material. In addition to the efficient handling of procedures, it is above all the safety aspect that is important to us, because the more severe the penalties for abuse and the faster the asylum procedures are handled, the more efficient asylum seekers can be integrated into Austria. In addition, the Foreign Pact makes the possibility of subjugated into illegality as well as of slipping into criminal behavioural patterns as much as possible difficult. All of this, in turn, is extremely important for the security of the Austrians. Therefore, international crime in the form of trafficking in human beings and towing will continue to be fought intensively in the coming year by new methods. We will also use the Presidency of Austria in the European Union to seek out opportunities for action in the European context and to seek the European asylum legislation as uniform as possible. I would like to thank the two ministers for their excellent work and I would like to agree with this package of foreign rights.

There has been a massive increase in the number of asylum seekers. It has also been pointed out at the time that we are at the forefront of the European countries in relation to the number of asylum applications. Already at that time, the diagnosis was made that this could no longer be considered as asylum in the narrower sense, but that, under the title “Asyl”, a massive immigration movement to Europe appears to take place. Secondly, it has been found that the organised crime of this instrument has also been authorised to live in the wealthy Europe. This can only be confirmed for today if we look at the figures and look at the countries according to the number of people who apply for asylum: Serbia-Montenegro is at the forefront, without any significant political events taking place there, laying the foundations for escape. The region of origin of the second largest group is the Russian Federation, then India, the world's largest democracy, then Turkey, which is seeking membership status in Europe. Quite clearly, the conclusion is that in the broadest sense it is no longer about refugees following the Geneva Refugee Convention, but about immigrants coming to us under this title. The conclusion that the Minister of the Interior has reached today is that it is necessary to make a clean separation between the protection against persecution in the asylum law and the exclusion of all those who come to us for other reasons is absolutely right. At that time it was claimed that this law could do all of this. If I follow up today's debate, I have to say: Everything has actually remained the same. The figures have fallen slightly across Europe, as Klubobmann Molterer said. As the Dublin procedure is now also applicable to the new EU countries, the problems have therefore become somewhat dilapidated. Austria is still at the forefront. The countries of origin are countries where no political events are taking place that could provide asylum grounds. It is a Déjà-vu experience: There has been no change since then, although it has been suggested that a suitable instrument has now been created to solve the problems and that asylum actually retains the protection of the people under the Geneva Convention on Refugees. This law is also presented by saying: Now we have solved the problem! – I hear the message well, only I know the bill and I therefore allow myself to doubt that it will succeed this time. [[Abg. Neudeck: But a step in the right direction is already!]] This for two reasons: Firstly, because this separation has not been carried out cleanly again – I will then prove it to a section – and secondly, because we continue to send massive incentives to come to us. This is a very unfortunate failure in view of the labour market situation. I also wonder why it is challenging to introduce controversial rules which only take place at the end of a long process, namely, if it is already impelled, into a law that – without underestimating the number – affects only relatively few people – about 1,000. At this time, for most of the years have passed, much of the tax money has been poured into the process. Two specific points to this: To separate asylum and immigration. . The Geneva Convention on Refugees proposes that asylum be examined regularly for reasons of asylum. She assumes, of course, that asylum is a protection on time. In Austria, on the other hand, this new law also applies: once asylum – always asylum. Asylum is almost necessarily transferred to citizenship after four or five years. This is a massive incentive for all those who cannot get through the immigration rate, successfully try it through the asylum rail and actually immigrate. As long as this remains so, I do not see that we will significantly reduce incentives. And for the second: The European Status Directive, which is to coordinate a common asylum policy, proposes in its Article 26, in its right and in a very realistic assessment of the effects, various rights – including access to the labour market – to the genuinely persecuted, the truly recognised refugee, after a positive conclusion of the asylum procedure. We – and we will certainly remain the only one, no other European country will do so – grant this right – namely access to the labour market – already to the asylum seekers, if within three months – practically never the case – his asylum application is not legally completed. This means a massive incentive for all those who want to work for higher wages and better conditions here – they have already been told; but we also have the right to protect our labour market. I wonder why it is not possible to do these simple things, which are absolutely in conformity with all international obligations, with European regulations. I really regret it – I regret it because this problem is one of the biggest in this country – to have to say that I am convinced that we have not brought anything efficient together this time and that the category of aggravation or non-enhancement is not a category that plays any role at all. Rather, it is about: Is this law effective? – And I fear and am convinced of: No, and so will not agree.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-07-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00116_d7e10586	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Asylum comes from the Greek “asylon” and means refuge. In earlier times, asylums were mostly sanctified places that protected refugees from access to secular power or jurisdiction. The legal philosophical justification of the concept of asylum currently refers to civil liberties, since some rights can only be fully guaranteed if citizens can choose their own human community. The right to leave this society must correspond with a right to cross into another. Many people use the right to seek a new home country in Europe. In 2004, 282 480 people in the European Union sought asylum, 8.7 percent of them in Austria alone. The focus of the new asylum law is therefore on rapid assistance for asylum seekers and the decision on whether or not they are actually recognized as refugees within the meaning of the Geneva Convention. If I listened to the SPÖ delegates and was always talking about compromise, I already have the feeling that they have somehow been orientationd by the great Michael Häupl, who thundered these days and made everyone tremble. I believe that we have, overall, a new asylum law, a balanced package here, by which, on the one hand, as before, people who need protection can get it quickly and on the other hand be excluded from abuse. Living together requires reliable conditions. We must take the people who live in this country seriously and take their fears seriously. I think we all know them. That is why it is a good law, and that is why we should all agree to it.

Citizens in this country have understanding and compassion for people who are in need. I remember the flow of refugees after World War II. My grandfather, my grandmother, my father, as well as hundreds of asylum-seeking SudetenGermans were taken into my small home town of Kautzen alone, and there was no decade in the last century in which Austria did not become a new home to Zig thousands of asylum seekers. Regrettably, it has to be noted that in recent years, internationally organised towbands have brought many people to Austria for financial reasons, who are not asylum seekers or even commit criminal acts. According to the latest data from the refugee organisation UNHCR, the number of asylum applications in the first quarter was 4,222 people. Germany, on the other hand, with a population of ten times higher than 6 000, recorded only a little more than 6 000 asylum applications. A seven-fold higher number of asylum seekers per head of our population in Germany shows that Austria has much more moderate legal regulations compared to the red-green government in Germany. Since autumn 2004, the Federal Ministry of Internal Affairs has been working on a balanced package involving all relevant locations, countries, the UNHCR and major NGOs to draw up a draft redefinition of the right to asylum, the right to asylum, the right to foreign and foreign police and the residence and establishment law of a comprehensive new regime. The main points of the present draft are: First, ensure that all those in need of protection receive this protection; Secondly, speed up the procedures; Third: to stop abuse and, where necessary, also to proceed with appropriate sanctions. The central issue in granting asylum is the reason for the escape, i.e. whether there are political, religious persecution or any other reasons for asylum described in the presentation. We are concerned about those who need help to grant them quickly and unbureaucratically. Austria should be a fair and valued country of asylum and remain. However, it is not intended to become a target country for international towing organisations wishing to do lucrative business. Ladies and gentlemen! It is certain that this is a very sensitive matter. In long and intensive discussions, many milder and sometimes stricter interpretations of the procedure were required. A good and common path has been found with the present government template.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-07-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00116_d7e11761	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! Mrs Haidlmayr! I'm allowed to give you the following: Today is a good day for civil service and especially for the Austrian civil servants. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Thank God, ladies and gentlemen, civil service has become an indispensable pillar in our social and health sectors in recent years. A kind of win-win situation has arisen, both for civil servants and for individual organisations, because we know that very high satisfaction exists in both. How did this civil service law-novelle come about? – For about a year, there has been a wide-ranging discussion about civil service in Austria. We owe this to our Federal Chancellor Dr. Wolfgang Schüssel, who called up a 42-member civilian service reform commission last year. There have been very intensive discussions and very intensive meetings on civil service. There have been similarities, but of course also different opinions. ==References====External links== A decision on the report and the recommendations was adopted by 25 : 15 votes in favour this year. That is, ladies and gentlemen, 62.5 percent! On this basis, Mrs Prokop, Federal Minister, has drawn up a government proposal. What are the essential contents or the most discussed points of this paper? – The main issue is the future duration of civil service. In this respect, our proposal and also the one in the Government's draft: nine months of compulsory service, three months of voluntary renewal. Why nine and not six months? – I should like to justify this, ladies and gentlemen. We have a number of suitable young men, about 35 000 a year. We know from the German army that, especially in the coming years, between 22,000 and 25,000 presenters per year are absolutely needed for the German army. We now have 10,000 civilian servants per year. If we cut civil service by half to six months, we would not need 10,000 civilians, but 20 000 civilians, so that we can maintain our health and social system as it is at present, and that is what we want. Anyone who masters the four basic methods of calculation knows that this simply cannot be calculated. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Another point is that rescue organizations in particular need very long training periods for civil servants, which last up to ten weeks. There are also decisions by Blue Light Organisations that establish that it is unthinkable for them to take civilian servants at a time under nine months of age. We take this into account because we want civil service to remain in these organisations, especially in the future. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] I would like to add a word to the SPÖ model. The SPÖ has finally proposed six plus three. That is to say, those who provide civil service to the Blue Light organizations are to make nine months' service, all the others six. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, don't be angry with me. This is discrimination within civil servants. We could not agree with that at the best of will. That's not possible, it's not feasible in practice. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The second point concerns voluntary renewal by three months. This is to be a real promotion measure for voluntary work. The civil servant receives 500 € per month during this voluntary extension. As an employee of the Red Cross, I know that there are many students and students who will be very much in use this year. It is really thought that this should be a promotion of voluntary work. A very important point and a very important improvement for our civil servants is the increase of the basic fee by 70 € to 256 €. In this way, they are drawn in the same way as the present servants. You should also mention 70 € more per month. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] This results in additional costs of € 8.8 million. That means that this federal government and the state of Austria are well worth the civil service. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] The Civil Service Agency will become a member of the Civil Service Agency. ==References====External links== I would like to thank the employees in the current Civil Service Administration GmbH. It's a great place to work. There is very rapid and good advice and support for civil servants. – Thank you very much to the staff of the Civil Service Management Agency, in the origin of the Civil Service Agency. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] A very important point is that, in future, the practice for civil servants, who are also active in the social and health field, can also be taken into account. [[Abg. Haidlmayr: What?]] There is still a GuKG novelle to follow, which needs to be adapted in the health field. I have already spoken to the Minister of the Interior. It shall signal consent in this respect. I think it is important that civil servants can be taken into account in practice. A controversial point on which Mrs Haidlmayr has been very intensively addressed is the point of view. We know that there are very different situations among support organisations. Many civil servants are being relocated or are introduced very cost-effectively. We have a motion for a resolution which says: As soon as the final knowledge of the Constitutional Court is available, the Minister will set a minimum limit on food allowances by regulation. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The voluntary opening up of social services for women is a major concern for us, especially with regard to civil society. There is also a motion for a resolution which states that a separate law on the promotion of voluntary social services should be created in relation to the already existing voluntary social year. I call for rapid implementation, because this parallel structure should be considered with a view to further change – that is, what happens to the obligation to do so – as early as 1. ==References====External links== Therefore, my great request is that the Minister of Social Affairs and the Federal Government should lead the necessary steps to bring about this law for the voluntary social year. This gives security to both the future civilian service providers and the many hundreds of support organisations. Thus they can plan, and they know what is ahead of them. ParlaMint-AT_2005-07-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00116_d7e12296	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! I would also like to comment briefly on this subject. I would like to stress that Civil service created by military service has long gained an independent meaning for social security and civil and social engagement in our country. We know that, and I can also emphasize that personally. Last year, 10,000 civil servants carried out their activities with 1 000 recognized institutions. I also know from my time as a social councillor how urgently civil servants are needed in various institutions today. I know what the civil servant is doing, I know what the aid, care and rescue organizations are doing, and I know that civil service is essential to the functioning of all of these things. – That is why I have addressed and defined three objectives at the start of my term of office. On the one hand, it is very important to maintain civil service at about the same level, in the same situation and in the order of magnitude as it is today in order to ensure social security as well. Secondly, it is necessary to create the best possible framework for civil service workers. Thirdly, there should be a reduction in the length of civil service in the event of a possible reduction in the service. However, we must also take account of and perceive the various interests in order not to break down this system which I have referred to at the outset. In other words, it is necessary to observe the need for support organisations, including with a view to establishing a viable relationship between training and working time. The requirements imposed on young men in terms of responsible handling of their lifetime, but also in terms of their abilities, must be taken into account, and in the end we must keep this in mind, above all, with regard to the future provision of such services in the event of the abolition of compulsory military service which is not currently under discussion. That is why there is also a legal proposal to ensure voluntary social service. It was therefore clear from the outset that it was not enough to shorten civil service. Rather, it must also be made much more attractive if its importance is to be maintained even under the new framework conditions. I would like to sum this up in six points: First: The financial performance to civilians must be increased and equated with the military service. This is a basic fee of 256 €. Secondly, The time of civil service is to be seen as a time taken for the individual. His personal experience, his additional qualifications, especially in terms of social competence, should be clearly and clearly documented. That is to say, a certificate of competence and a certificate of practice are to be introduced. In the future – and we need to continue working on this – the knowledge and skills acquired can then also be used in practical life. In this regard, we are in discussions with the countries, but also with the Ministry of Health and Social Affairs, in order to achieve a possible credit for future training, for example in the social and disability sectors, in the elderly and in the health care sector. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Perhaps we can achieve the third goal, namely that more young men should become more involved in social work, because we will need it in the future in order to be able to maintain the care sector effectively, possibly later also through voluntary social benefits. Fourthly, there is also an incentive for voluntary engagement now, i.e. following civil service, there is the possibility of an extension of three months in which the person concerned has a social security scheme and the promotion of volunteers is increased to €500. In addition, we want to facilitate the delivery of the service. We aim for more flexible and additional allocation dates so that career and study can be better planned and, above all, the voluntary extension can be made easier. [[Bravorufe and congratulation with the ÖVP and with representatives of the liberals.]] The Civil Service Agency will also be more concerned with the idea of service for civil service providers, but also for the institutions, and it will also take this into account. Fifth, the duration of civil service. I think that the reduction to nine months, i.e. one quarter of the present time, is absolutely justified, because the institutions too have to cope with this transitional period. We must bear in mind that we now need a third more civilian servants to maintain today's status and to be able to ensure the benefits I initially addressed. This means that we will continue to need a sufficient number of trained civil servants in the future. The sixth point, which I would like to address, of course also concerns the improvement of institutions, particularly social and disability facilities. Ladies and gentlemen! I would also like to thank you for this recast. I would like to thank the members of the Civil Service Reform Commission, chaired by President Fredy Mayer, for the work done: A very comprehensive report has been drawn up. Above all, however, I would like to thank all the representatives of the relief, care and rescue facilities involved for their constructive and critical contributions as well as for their advice. Finally, I would like to thank officials, above all, for the consistent and professional implementation of this package, which has been worked on here. Ladies and gentlemen! I would particularly like to thank the parliamentary groups for their active participation in this renewal process. We have tried to examine all opinions seriously, to include them in the decision-making process and to implement them as far as possible. As provided for in the Constitution, civil service is shortened on the basis of a broad parliamentary consensus and at the same time made much more attractive. I therefore believe – and I am convinced – that an important contribution to social security can also be made in the interests of civil servants. I would like to thank you once again for the broad consensus.

The present Growth and Employment Act 2005 is the consistent continuation of a successful economic policy strategy since the political turn of 2000. I should like to point out that this government and this Parliament each year have provided impetus to strengthen growth and employment: This was done in 2001 by the I economic package, in 2002 by the II economic package, in 2003 by the growth and location package, in 2004 by the first stage of the tax reform, in 2005 by the second stage of the tax reform and now through this 2005 employment and growth package. Ladies and gentlemen, this is not a coincidence! That's what we see when we compare the numbers today. Let us draw a comparison with our neighbouring country, which is still our most important trading partner. It is not a coincidence that we have twice as high growth, half such unemployment and half the deficit. Rather, this is the consequence of this economic policy, ladies and gentlemen, which each year has tried to set up impulses against an international trend, which is very difficult for a small country. We know that there is a weak growth in Europe, and we know that we need to counter this. I've already listed it: This federal government has set the impetus each year, and that is why we have much better economic data than comparable countries, ladies and gentlemen. I would like to highlight two points in this package. First point: We are aware that the most dynamic growth factor is research, development and innovation.
